# PLANNED / BACKLOG — every unbuilt-but-intended thing, in one place

> **Companion to `CURRENT-STATE.md`.** That file says what IS; this one says what is **intended and
> not yet done**. Assembled 2026-08-26 (Prompt 141) by sweeping every design brief, prompt
> "follow-ups" section, gap-audit tail, status ledger and archived STATUS block in the repo.
>
> **Nothing here is a new idea.** Every row cites **where it came from** so you can read the original
> reasoning, and so a future consolidation can prove nothing was invented or dropped. Rows are kept
> even when they look dead — *a contemplated feature is never deleted, only re-ranked or explicitly
> retired with a reason.*
>
> **Before building any row: apply the P131 lens.** Classify the gap first —
> **(a)** the answer is already on-box and STRUCTURED → deterministic plumbing, **not** an LLM;
> **(b)** on-box but UNSTRUCTURED text → an LLM fits; **(c)** not on-box at all → neither, an LLM
> would fabricate. Two of the top-ranked local-model gaps turned out to be (a) and (c) once measured.
>
> **And re-measure the row before you build it.** Several rows below carry dates from July; the
> standing doctrine is that a dated blocker is a hypothesis, not a fact.

**Legend** — 🔴 not started · 🟡 partially built · 🟢 designed/specced, ready to build ·
⏳ built, pending a manual apply/flip · 👤 needs Scott (tenant / human decision) · ⚪ optional/roadmap ·
🔍 needs re-measure before it can be ranked honestly.

---

## P0 — Verify, don't build (open loops on work already shipped)

These are the cheapest and highest-trust items: something shipped, and the **delta has not been
confirmed**. This repo's own rule is that a fix is not fixed until the population moves.

| ✅ **TEST-NET-LEAK** | **`npm test` made 14 live HTTPS calls to the production `ai-copilot/chat` edge function on every run** — measured 2026-09-09 two ways: `function_edge_logs` on Dialysis_DB showed 19 bursts of exactly 14 `POST 400` in 3-minute windows from rotating Azure (GitHub-runner) addresses in 24 h plus 71 from a developer machine; a local run of the full suite under a `fetch`-logging shim reproduced exactly 14: `test/lease-extractor.test.mjs` (8) and `test/dossier-generator.test.mjs` (6). Cause: both tests assumed "no AI key → extractor throws", but `api/_shared/ai.js` `invokeChatProvider` defaults `AI_EXTRACTION_PRIMARY` to `edge`, which needs no key and POSTs to the live function unauthenticated; the 400 was swallowed by the fallback chain and the assertion never saw the network. **SHIPPED 2026-09-09**: `test/_helpers/net-guard.mjs` (`--import`ed ahead of every test file, wraps `fetch`, throws on any non-loopback host) + `invokeChatProvider` refuses the `edge` route before fetching under `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT`/`LCC_HERMETIC_TESTS`. Neither leaking test file needed rewriting — their assertions already describe *provider failure*, which the seam now returns deterministically. Guard `test/hermetic-suite.test.mjs`. Full suite re-verified: ~~5,563 / 5,557 pass / 0 fail / 0 live calls, 67s~~ **Cowork re-measured from the merged tree under the original fetch-logging shim: 5,568 / 5,562 pass / 0 fail / 6 skipped / 0 live calls / 181 s vs 376 s before** (faster — no more 35s backoff sleeps against a dead route; CI's `npm test` check shortens the same way). Response: `docs/claude-code/responses/TEST-NET-LEAK-hermetic-suite.response.md`. | ✅ | Cowork 2026-09-09 |

| 🟡 **RAILWAY-PA-SECRET** | 👤 **`PA_WEBHOOK_SECRET` appears to be UNSET on the tranquil-delight Railway service** (Derived 2026-09-09: after the COPILOT-OPEN-gate Railway redeploy, every Railway→`ai-copilot` call logs `DENY-WOULD … node railway`, and the new `connectorHeaders()`/`handleCopilotRead` attach the header only when the variable is present). Consequence beyond the copilot gate: `api/sync.js::authenticateWebhook` returns `true` for everyone when the variable is absent ("transitional"), so the seven PA webhook routes on Railway (`rcm-ingest`, `rcm-backfill`, `loopnet-ingest`, `processing-complete`, `todo-completion-poll`, `listing-webhook`, `cross-domain-match`) are unauthenticated on the live app (`lead-ingest`/`live-ingest` proxy straight to the edge function or use plain `authenticate()` — no `authenticateWebhook` path, so they are not in this count). §4a of `AI-SURFACES-OPERATIONAL-REFERENCE.md` said "already set" — that was Supabase's secret list, not Railway's. **Exports read 2026-09-09 (17 flows, 51 HTTP actions): NO flow sends the secret to Railway — they send `x-lcc-key` or a bearer, which the handlers' fallback (`authenticate()` + operator role) accepts once the variable is set. Breakage on set = callers sending neither: the To Do Completion Poll's second, header-less call, plus the three UNEXPORTED PA5 flows (RCM Email Watcher, LoopNet, Personal Calendar Sync — headers Not on file). Whether `LCC_API_KEY` is set on Railway is also Not on file.** ✅ **RAILWAY-PA-SECRET-log SHIPPED 2026-09-10 (re-run — a prior CC session finished this unit correctly but its branch never reached `origin` and no PR was opened; that work is unrecoverable and this is a clean re-run, not a continuation).** `api/sync.js::webhookAuth()` is now the single gate for all seven call sites (`test/pa-webhook-auth-mode.test.mjs`, 9 tests; full suite 5,603/0/6-skipped). `PA_WEBHOOK_AUTH_MODE` defaults to `log`: with the secret unset today, nothing changes; once Scott sets `PA_WEBHOOK_SECRET` on Railway, a caller the fallback would also deny is logged `[pa-webhook] DENY-WOULD <route> <fallback-path> <ua_class> <ip_class>` and still let through, never refused, until `PA_WEBHOOK_AUTH_MODE=enforce`. **👤 Operator order, in this sequence:** (1) merge this PR → redeploy Railway; (2) set `PA_WEBHOOK_SECRET` to the same value Dialysis_DB holds (+ `PA_WEBHOOK_KNOWN_IPS` if the known caller IPs are known — same `class:prefix` format as `COPILOT_KNOWN_IPS`); (3) read `[pa-webhook] DENY-WOULD … none` for ~3 days — a `none` line is the only thing that predicts a break; (4) fix each `none` caller, starting with the To Do Completion Poll flow's second, header-less call (designer edit + re-export, never hand-edit the JSON) and the three unexported PA5 flows; (5) flip `PA_WEBHOOK_AUTH_MODE=enforce`. | 🟡 👤 | COPILOT-OPEN · Cowork 2026-09-09 |
| ✅ **CFE-RUNAWAY** | **Root cause named and fixed in code 2026-09-10, in `Dialysis` (repo this session cannot reach directly) — merged and auto-deployed, confirmed by Scott 2026-09-10, and independently confirmed holding live: a 2026-09-10 16:27 UTC log excerpt shows every `supabase_execute_wrapper` call at `count=1`, no recurrence of the full-table probes.** Exact call site, per the CC session's own report: `src/financial_estimate_tracker.py:463-472`, `FinancialEstimateTracker._pk_column()`, called once per `save_estimate()` (i.e. once per clinic) with `get_live_table_columns(TABLE_NAME, force_refresh=True)` — `force_refresh=True` bypassed the schema cache and fell through to the exact two unfiltered probes measured live (`select=*&limit=1`, `select=created_at&limit=1`). Fix: cache the PK column on the tracker instance (one per run), never pass `force_refresh=True` — drops the probe from once-per-record to at most once-per-run. Two adjacent defects fixed alongside it: `properties.estimated_annual_revenue` propagation (was silently dropping the field via a `None` module global — client now threaded through) and an "Invalid propagation target" warning that fired unconditionally even on success (a test had pinned the bug; removed). Five `facility_patient_counts` field drops decided per-field: 4 intentional (methodology diagnostics that belong on `clinic_financial_estimates` instead), 1 (`revenue_medicaid`) a real schema gap — migration filed for review, not applied. Retention (Unit 5) deliberately **not executed** — proposal only, needs a live count query. **`B6d-cms-restart` checked and reported NOT the same mechanism** — `save_estimate()` already catches its own timeouts, so this alone isn't what was killing CMS runs; that question stays open. Full `Dialysis` suite reported 3,153/0 (their own CI invocation). **PR opened: `sbriggssjc/Dialysis#7398`, branch `claude/cfe-runaway-financial-estimates-b913114d` — not yet confirmed merged.** ⚠️ **Independently observed (Supabase logs, this session, 2026-09-10 ~15:14 UTC): statement timeouts on Dialysis_DB dropped from ~400/h to 2 in a 20-minute window, and zero `python-httpx` traffic to `clinic_financial_estimates` in the prior 24 minutes** — consistent with either the fix being live, or the service simply being paused/idle between passes. **This is NOT proof the fix is deployed** — confirm PR #7398's merge state and whether the Railway service was paused separately before crediting the drop to the code fix. Full writeup: `docs/claude-code/responses/done/CFE-RUNAWAY-cms-financial-estimates-repair.response.md`. | 🟡 👤 | Cowork 2026-09-09/10 |
| ✅ **RATINGS-INSERT-COLLISION** | 👤 **CONFIRMED LIVE IN PRODUCTION 2026-09-11 — this session independently queried Dialysis_DB directly (not from Scott's uploaded log excerpt, which didn't reach the ratings phase) and found the real production cutover.** `ingestion_tracker` shows a `cms_medicare_clinics` run starting **2026-09-10 22:05:31 UTC** — after `RATINGS3`'s PR #7402 merged at 18:32:10 UTC — and a direct query of `ratings` shows **all 7,013 rows now carry a fresh `updated_at` between 22:05:32 and 22:17:13 UTC that same run**, up from the March 2026-03-12 baseline that had been stuck through three straight rounds. Zero `ratings_medicare_id_uidx` duplicate-key errors from 22:19 UTC onward through the next 8 hours (checked minute-by-minute in `postgres_logs`) — this is the first time in this saga a full production run has actually landed on the fixed code and succeeded end to end, not just a single hand-run row. **One residual item, not blocking**: during the transition itself (22:05:32–22:17:13), ~219 `ratings_medicare_id_uidx` duplicate-key errors still fired even as all 7,013 rows ultimately succeeded — declining from ~18–21/min down to 0, consistent with either the old and new container briefly overlapping during the actual redeploy cutover, or a benign retry-then-succeed race under concurrency; worth a quick look next time logs come in, but it did not stop a single row from writing. Scott's 2026-09-11 06:16 UTC run (uploaded logs) had not yet reached the ratings-ingestion phase as of the log excerpt provided (only 19 seconds of CFE-RUNAWAY/PROPREV1-stage activity captured) — this ✅ verdict rests on the 22:05 UTC run's live database state, independently queried, not on that excerpt. **Background — how the arc got here:** RATINGS3's Unit 2 found the real explanation for the two prior "still broken" verdicts: the 2026-09-10 test run judged RATINGS2 against started at **17:23:08 UTC, 69 minutes before RATINGS2's own merge at 18:32:10 UTC**, with a flat duplicate-key error rate straight through the merge instant — the running container was executing pre-merge code the entire time ("merged is not running," not a code defect). RATINGS3's Unit 3 then proved the fix live and directly, no mock, against a single row (`id=1`/`medicare_id='012500'`) — no `42P10`, no `23505`, a genuine UPDATE. It also found and fixed a real defect in the process: `ratings.updated_at` had never been stamped by either write path (no update trigger either), so the metric this whole arc used to judge success (`max(updated_at)`) was structurally blind to a real write — now stamped explicitly, gated on column existence. **PR `sbriggssjc/Dialysis#7402` confirmed merged, `main` at `000eda1`; the 2026-09-10 22:05 UTC run is the first production run to actually execute that commit, and it worked at full-table scale.** Full writeup: `docs/claude-code/responses/done/RATINGS3-live-proven-upsert-fix.response.md`. | ✅ | Cowork 2026-09-11 |
| ✅ **PROPREV1** | **CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-09-10 — the first fix in this arc independently verified working, not just tested.** Root cause: CFE-RUNAWAY's client-threading fix WAS present and correct — the real bug was one layer downstream, in `column_exists(refresh=True)` (`src/schema_guards.py:208`), which preferred the on-disk schema cache whenever non-empty, ignoring the live client regardless of `refresh=True`. Fixed by skipping that cache shortcut when `refresh=True`, threading the client explicitly into `propagate_financials_to_properties()`. Test gap also confirmed and closed (CFE-RUNAWAY's 8 tests stubbed the real chain out; a new 4-test file exercises it, RED/GREEN). **Live proof, 2026-09-10: `properties.estimated_annual_revenue`'s null count dropped from 5,113 to 4,840 (273 real writes) across one run** — the exact metric named as the open question, now closed. `clinic_financial_estimates` grew normally in the same run, no runaway-probe recurrence. PR `sbriggssjc/Dialysis#7400`. Full writeup: `docs/claude-code/responses/done/PROPREV1-estimated-annual-revenue-propagation-still-dropped.response.md`. | ✅ | Cowork 2026-09-10 |

| ✅ **PRI2** | 👤 **Closed — superseded by `PRI3`, now fixed/merged/deployed.** Root-cause escalation finding, now confirmed far more serious than first framed — see `PRI3` for the full, devastating inventory found in a live crash the same day; that inventory is now fixed, merged (`sbriggssjc/Dialysis#7406`), and deployed live, so this row is closed rather than left open pointing at a resolved item. `src.supabase_execute_wrapper`'s calls were first observed failing their first attempt 50% of the time (retry-then-succeed, just wasteful) in one run. A second run, hours later, showed the same root cause causing **actual permanent data loss and zero-output pipeline stages** — not just wasted retries — because several call sites have no retry at all. Same error signature (`RemoteProtocolError: ConnectionTerminated`) in `public_record_ingest.py` (fixed, `PRI1`), `cms_aux_ingestion` (retries, wasteful but survives), `oig_leie_ingestor`, `ownership_linker`, `utils_shared`, and `ingestion_tracker` (all broken, no/insufficient retry — see `PRI3`) strongly indicates one shared root cause (the underlying httpx/HTTP2 client or connection pooling — e.g. a pooled connection Supabase's edge has already reset getting reused on first use), not six independent bugs. `PRI3`'s response addressed the root-cause question directly: **not determinable from this repo** (no lockfile/Dockerfile/Railway config committed to audit actual dependency versions, no deliberate version bump in git history) — the call-site `safe_execute()` approach was the practical path forward, and that approach is now shipped. | ✅ | Cowork 2026-09-11, closed — superseded by PRI3 |

(a) `oig_leie_ingestor`, (b) all 9 `ownership_linker` sub-steps, (d) `utils_shared`'s `pending_updates` fetch, (e) `ingestion_tracker`'s `start_run` — all reported fixed via the same `safe_execute()` pattern `PRI1` proved correct (e's fix included a self-caught regression: moving query construction outside `safe_execute()` broke a test using an incomplete stub client, fixed by wrapping construction+execution in a lambda). (c) the `owners` `UnboundLocalError` — confirmed a genuine, separate Python bug (unassigned local variable referenced after a preceding step failed), fixed independent of the retry work. Full suite: **3183 passed** (up from 3173), 0 failed. work; this one measures the funnel.** gov: 13,835 live properties → 6,362 with an LCC owner link →

to the developer across target markets* — **that 1.1% is the number that matters.**

| ⭐ **B6d** | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-29 — and the two alerts everyone assumed were mis-sized SLAs are REAL BREAKS** ([`docs/audits/B6d_FEED_EXPECTATION_GRADING_2026-08-29.md`](../audits/B6d_FEED_EXPECTATION_GRADING_2026-08-29.md)). Fixed with **B6a's own lesson one layer up: an unwatched feed EMITS**, with a NULL bound as a *positive* statement, and a resolve arm keyed on that — **never on ABSENCE**, which also covers a feed whose query errored or whose mirror went blind. The residual is **counted as `alerts_orphaned`, never auto-resolved**: a decision and a disappearance must not close identically. **Retire the BOUND, never the ROW.** ⚠️ **THE POPULATION IS 25, NOT 23** — LCC Opps carries its own registry (`om_intake`, `salesforce_sync`), invisible to a count taken from the domain DBs. ⚠️ **RULE 3c HELD TWICE.** dia `medicare_clinics` (65d/45d): p50 gap 2d, **max gap ever 41d**, so 45 was never the problem — `ingestion_tracker` shows **27 failed + 6 abandoned** CMS runs since the last success 2026-06-25 while `dataset_modified_date` reads **2026-08-25**; widening would have buried a two-month outage against a LIVE source (**B6d-cms**). gov `sam_lease_opportunities` re-scoped 14 → 21 **and deliberately left violated at 33d**: the weekly producer is fine (`usajobs`, same workflow, landed 2026-08-24) and the SAM call itself returns **401 Unauthorized** (**B6d-sam**). ⚠️ **That 401 is NOT the §18 rate limit** — different key, different endpoint; §18's *"the 401 is not real"* correction is exactly what makes a real 401 easy to dismiss. ⚠️ **MEASURING A FEED'S OWN GAPS IS CIRCULAR WHEN IT HAS BEEN DEAD**: an outage is a CLOSED gap and enters its own distribution — `gsa_lease_change_facts` has 2 dates and one 170d gap (*the outage B6b repaired*), so 3×p90 derives a **510-day** bound; and a lifetime window mixes ERAS (`usajobs` p90 31.8/max 95 over life, p50 7/max 16 in the scheduled era). **B6a's p90 rule does not transfer from steps to feeds.** Primary basis = the producer's DECLARED schedule; below 3 gaps the honest verdict is `cannot_be_sized_from_data`, recorded not dressed up. **Weekly family → 21 = 3× cadence** (6 feeds at 14/30/35/45; 14 had already produced a false fire). ⚠️ **The GSA family is FOUR feeds, ONE publisher, and carried FOUR bounds** (65/35/45/45), three below the cycle's own peak: publication is monthly with a **21–51d lag**, peak data age ~82d → `gsa_leases_snapshot` **65 → 90** (it was **6 days from firing** on a healthy feed, with `consecutive_unchanged=3` proving GSA has not published August), derived trio **→ 75**, pinned by a guard so they cannot drift apart again. `gsa_lease_events` was 35 and **would have fired 2026-09-10**: its cadence changed 2026-08-10 when the fingerprint dedupe began skipping, and its bound had not. **`gsa_source_pull` stays tight at 21** — *did WE stop pulling* is a different question from *is GSA publishing*. **`opm_workforce` 120 → 200**: data is 74–75d old **at the moment of a successful import** and the one observed import interval is 119d, so 120 was **unmeetable by the process that feeds it** — it fired 3× in 3 months, every one closed "expected". Controls (rolled back): opm@199d→0 alerts, opm@205d→1, gsa@95d→1, unwatched@1800d→0, orphan-counter→1 named. Guards: LCC 7 tests **12/12 mutations RED**, gov 8 tests **14/14 RED**. ⚠️ **A REVIEW BOT CAUGHT A SECURITY CLAIM THIS ROUND ASSERTED WITHOUT POSITIVE-CONTROLLING IT** (Codex, on Dialysis#7378): `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM anon, authenticated` on `compute_feed_cadence` was a **no-op** — Postgres grants EXECUTE on a new FUNCTION to **PUBLIC** by default, so both roles still reached the SECURITY DEFINER function; measured live after the "fix", `proacl` carried the leading `=X/postgres` PUBLIC grant and `has_function_privilege('anon', oid, 'EXECUTE')` read TRUE on gov and dia. | **B6d-pri-metrics** | 🟡 **`metrics.persist_run_summary` defines an inner `_insert()` and NEVER CALLS IT.** The function writes a `run_log` event and **no summary row anywhere** — so every caller that believes it is persisting a run summary is persisting nothing. | **B6d-cms-escalation** | 🔴 **The producer died repeatedly and nothing escalated — and it is structural, not an oversight.** This pipeline is in **no producer registry**: its runs land in `ingestion_tracker`, which **no health view consumes**, while B6a's `run_log` → `v_pipeline_task_health` surface is gov-side. So the **45-day freshness bound was the ONLY thing that could ever catch it**, and it took two months. **A failing producer should be louder than a stale table** — the freshness monitor measures a *symptom*; nothing watched the *cause*. This is **B6a Class 21 one layer up**: B6a fixed *"a skipped step emits nothing"*; here **the step emits, into a table with no consumer.** Cheapest honest version: alert on any row reaching `abandoned`, and on `max(started_at) − max(started_at where run_status='success')` exceeding the feed's own cadence. Not built with the fix, deliberately — bundling a new detector would make it impossible to tell which change moved the number. | 🔴 | B6d-cms §6 |
| **B6d-cms-lock** | 🟡 **`acquire_ingestion_lock` can reclaim the pipeline's OWN rows.** `ingest_medicare_clinics` calls it with `force=force or force_refresh`, so any forced run force-reclaims every `started` row for the dataset — **including the tracker rows the same run opened seconds earlier** — and marks them `failed`. That is the origin of the *"Reclaimed after 0.0h stale"* rows. Root shape: the lock writes its own row into `ingestion_tracker` under the **same `dataset_id`**, so lock rows and pipeline run rows are indistinguishable to its own query. B6d-cms fixed only the **message** (it hardcoded `"stale"` regardless of reason — a self-contradicting *"0.0h stale"* that cost a diagnosis cycle). The semantics are untouched: it is lock behaviour, not the outage, and deserves its own change. | 🟡 | B6d-cms §7 |

## P0s — ⏸️ SECRETS EXPOSED IN THE REPO — ROTATION DEFERRED BY DECISION (Scott, 2026-08-29)

> 👤 **DECISION, 2026-08-29: all credential rotation is deferred until a second user is added to
> LCC.** Rationale: single-user, still building, private repo — the exposure has no second party to
> reach. **This is a recorded risk acceptance with a trigger, not an oversight**, and the rows stay
> open so it cannot go quiet.
>
> 🔔 **The trigger is broader than "another user" — the PRIVATE REPO is what carries this risk.**
> Rotate BEFORE any of: a second LCC user · **making the repo public** · **sharing it with a
> contractor, vendor or Northmarq IT** · a lost/compromised laptop · LCC leaving "still building"
> for anything a client touches.
>
> ✅ **SEC4 STAYS ACTIVE.** It is a guard, not a rotation: deferring rotation is a decision about the
> keys already exposed; **SEC4 is what stops the next PA export adding more.** Without it, the
> rotation you eventually do is against a moving target.
>
> ⚠️ **When rotation happens, ORDER MATTERS: rotate → update Railway → `git rm --cached` +
> `.gitignore` → only then consider history.** `git rm --cached` alone leaves the value in history
> and in every clone. **Do not reach for `filter-branch`** — this repo nearly lost a 475 MB mailbox
> that way.

⚠️ **These are credential exposures, not hygiene. They outrank every other row in this file.**
**Rotation is what neutralises them — removing a file from tracking does NOT**, because the value
remains in git history and in any clone or fork.

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🚨 **SEC2** | **`wave0-config-values.txt` is TRACKED IN GIT at the repo root and contains `LCC_API_KEY` in PLAINTEXT.** Verified 2026-08-28: 858 bytes, `git ls-files` confirms tracked, **not in `.gitignore`**. Also carries `LCC_HOST=https://life-command-center-nine.vercel.app` (**the retired host that still answers and still holds a service key**, P194) plus Teams tenant/team/channel IDs. ⚠️ **This is a DIFFERENT and worse exposure than SEC1**, which records the key as *"pasted in plaintext during a curl diagnostic"* — a chat paste is transient; **a tracked file is in history, in every clone, and in every fork.** **Order: (1) ROTATE `LCC_API_KEY` and update the Railway env; (2) `git rm --cached` + `.gitignore`; (3) only then consider history.** ⚠️ **Do NOT reach for `filter-branch` casually** — this repo has a documented near-loss of a 475 MB mailbox doing exactly that (`CLAUDE.md`, destructive-op order). **Rotation alone makes the committed value worthless, which is the outcome that matters.** | 🔴 | `wave0-config-values.txt:6` |
| 🚨 **SEC3** | **TEN OF SEVENTEEN deployed Power Automate packages contain literal JWT-like values**, per `docs/os/POWER-AUTOMATE-DEPLOYED-CATALOG.md`. `docs/architecture/flows/sync-sf-activities-to-supabase.md` carries an unresolved **P0**: *"plaintext credential material detected in exported definition — rotate exposed Supabase keys immediately"*, with **`Credential rotation completed: TBD`**. ⚠️ **I1 covers only the `X-PA-Webhook-Secret`; the Supabase key rotation across those ten packages is a SEPARATE and larger item that was never filed.** Establish which keys, whether any are service-role, and rotate. | 🔴 | flows catalog |
| **SEC4** | **Every PA flow export committed to this repo is a credential-bearing artefact by default.** SEC2 and SEC3 are two instances of one pattern. **Add a pre-commit or CI check for JWT-shaped / `sb_secret_` / long-hex strings in `*.json` flow exports and `*.txt` config files** — the guard this repo has for conflict markers, applied to secrets. Without it the next export re-introduces the same exposure silently. | 🔴 | derived |

## P0d — 🏛️ DATA COHERENCE: make the connections assertable, across every database

**Scott's standing requirement, 2026-08-28:** *"eliminate these exact types of code and/or logical
gaps or misconnections… in all our databases (current two and planned others) so that all data
sources and ingestion is propelling the entire database forward, not just a bunch of different
component parts or subdatabases or tables."*

📍 **Contract:** [`docs/architecture/data-coherence-invariants.md`](../architecture/data-coherence-invariants.md)
— ten invariants (I1–I10), the new-database onboarding checklist, and the honest detector status:
**two of ten invariants have a standing detector.** Everything else was found by a human asking the

📍 **The ownership → true-owner → CRM pipeline specifically** (property → recorded owner → chain-to-developer → true owner → Salesforce/Outlook/WebEx → LCC pushed back out): [`docs/architecture/ownership-truth-pipeline-state.md`](../architecture/ownership-truth-pipeline-state.md) — written 2026-09-10, walks all five stages against this file's own row ids, names the two cross-stage patterns (entity-dedup is the shared upstream blocker; several Decision Center lanes sit unworked, not unbuilt).
right question. **These rows turn the highest-yield ones into scheduled checks.**

⚠️ **Every defect behind this campaign was individually correct code.** Component tests, boot checks
and health views all passed. **These are properties of CONNECTIONS, which nothing currently asserts.**

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **D1** | **I2 — the provenance producer-set diff, as a standing view.** The single highest-yield query in the system: group a fact store by its provenance column, split by domain; **a bucket present for one domain and absent for another IS the finding**, no hypothesis needed. It found B5 (gov never consumed its own sales table — 2,776 rows / 2,000 properties / **677 with no prior history at all**). **Run it over every shared fact store, not just ownership.** ⚠️ **A difference is not automatically a defect** — dia has no GSA lease inventory and that is legitimate. The view must let a difference be **explained and acknowledged**, or it becomes a badge of noise. | 🔴 | Class 20 · ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-29** — `scripts/d1-cross-db-provenance-diff.mjs` + planner `api/_shared/provenance-diff-planner.js` + ledger `scripts/d1-provenance-acknowledgements.json` + guard `test/d1-cross-db-provenance-diff.test.mjs` (18 tests, **19 mutations verified RED**). Writeup: [`D1`](../audits/D1_CROSS_DB_PROVENANCE_DIFF_2026-08-29.md). **Re-run MONTHLY** and on adding any source or domain DB. **First run: 69 differences over 23 two-sided stores — 58 legitimate, 5 unexplained, 6 unwired, NONE B5-sized.** ⚠️ **That is the real result: the two domains are substantially coherent, and the detector's value is now preventing the next divergence rather than closing a current one.** ⚠️ **THE BIGGER FINDING IS A PRECONDITION NOBODY STATED — 12 tables exist in both domains and record provenance in only ONE, so I2 cannot be evaluated on them at all**; the one that matters is **dia `ownership_history` (10,037 rows, no provenance column)** — the very store B5 was about (**D1g**). ⚠️ Five ways the query returns a confident wrong answer, all hit live and all now guarded: per-domain column NAMES (`properties` = `data_source`/`source`), a **dead** second column (gov `property_financials.source`, **0 of 98,510**), per-row suffixes, a provenance column holding a DATA VALUE at modest cardinality (`ingestion_tracker.source` = temp paths), and one producer wearing two labels. ⚠️ **NOT scheduled** — a job that has never run green once is the badge people merge past (**D1h**); the script's I/O path is **unexercised** because the sandbox holds no domain credentials. |

and *the lease states no rent* keeps that flag **false**, because it is a different fact from *we cannot convert the rent it states* (P180). **Consumer contract unchanged** — the six graded keys keep their names and types, `rent_schedule` keeps `annual_rent`, and a bare legacy number or date string still resolves, so **no backfill**. ⚠️ **PREDICTED, not measured:** `year1_rent` self-rate → ~100%, `lease_expiration` → rises. Every override records its source. Three decisions worth knowing: the basis window stops at the NEXT `$` (a parenthetical monthly restatement no longer abstains the row); amount is presence-in-the-quote, never a tolerance (8.7965 vs 8,796.50 is 1,000×, indistinguishable from a different figure by any threshold); a formula is never turned into a date even when it contains one (`parseStatedDate` consumes the whole quote after a closed wrapper set). 23 guards, 16/16 mutations RED. *(Scope as staged:)* Parse `as_stated` in CODE as the authority for rent basis and date precision; the model's labels become the fallback. The EXT1 floor run (`responses/done/EXT1-floor-measurement.response.md`) shows the quotes are reliably verbatim while the labels beside them are not: doc 431 `as_stated: "$8,796.50 per month"` labelled `per_sf_annual` with `amount 8.7965` → null; doc 336 `as_stated` carries the rent schedule with `amount null`; a plain `"March 15, 2021"` labelled `formula` on one of two runs. Rules: `per month|monthly` → monthly; `per year|per annum|annually` → annual; `/sf|per square foot` → per-sf; first `$` figure when the quote is a schedule; a full calendar date → `precision: day` + parsed date. Guard fixtures = those three named rows. Metric: the `--control self` floor on `year1_rent` and both dates (89 / 80 / 80 today) → ~100 on this sample. Measure the disagreement rate between the two stoplists before touching either — the CoStar scanner's list may simply be the more correct one, in which case the fix is to have the backend read it, not to widen a third regex. owner-role-classification.md §9h. | 🟡 | C13g |
| ✅ **C13g-costar-stoplist** | **✅ SHIPPED 2026-09-10 — the "never reads the scanner's own verdict back" framing this row started with was WRONG; traced and fixed the real gap.** `contactEntityType()` DOES check `contact.type` first, and CoStar's `_forsale-contacts-parse.js::looksLikePerson()` DOES always stamp an explicit `type` — so its verdict wins outright, `hasFirmSuffix()` was never consulted on this path, and the two lists were never really "two independently drifting copies": `hasFirmSuffix()` already covers almost the entire extension stoplist (Trust/Holdings/Properties/Capital/Realty/Ventures/Management/Company), missing only 4 brokerage brand names (newmark/cbre/jll/colliers) the extension has. **The real defect ran the OTHER direction: the extension's list is missing terms `hasFirmSuffix()` has** — Bancorp, Investments, Development/Developers, Fund, Ptnrs, Cos, Property (singular), Enterprises, Mgmt-abbrev — so a name like "Sentinel Bancorp" or "Meridian Investments" trips the backend's guard but not the extension's, and the extension stamps `type:'person'` on it, which — before this fix — was trusted verbatim and would mint the firm as a person entity. Fix (case **(a)**, but inverted from the row's original framing): `contactEntityType()` now treats an explicit `type:'person'` as a FLOOR, not an absolute — `hasFirmSuffix(name)` still overrides it to `'organization'`, one-way only (an explicit `'organization'`/`'entity'` is never second-guessed, matching the P158a discipline against guessing an org into a person on a heuristic). No second stoplist created; no extension code touched (same precedent as RCA in C13g). Guard: `test/c13g-contact-entity-type.test.mjs` (11 tests, all pass) — added `Sentinel Bancorp`/`Meridian Investments`/`Ashford Development` cases and updated the "explicit type wins" assertion, which the old code would have failed (it asserted `ACME LLC` + `type:'person'` → `'person'`, the exact bug). Forward-mint only, same as C13g — existing mistyped population is `entity_type_review` lane material, not bulk-retyped here. owner-role-classification.md §9i (corrects §9h's wrong "never reads it back" claim). | ✅ | C13g · owner-role-classification.md §9i · test/c13g-contact-entity-type.test.mjs |

## P1c — Multi-party ownership: JV, fund and partnership structures (Scott, 2026-08-27)

**New requirement, raised while reviewing V8.** Scott: *"I think we connect JV LLC or SPEs like we
do recorded owner to LLC/SPE to true owner, but link to **multiple true owners** for each true owner
in the JV. Partnerships are going to be a good example of this down the road. We will have
investors that own assets outright, in JV and maybe in a fund like a DST or something similar."*

**This is a data-model change, not a lane fix**, and it is the first requirement in this arc that
the current model cannot express. Today the chain is single-valued: `recorded owner → SPE → ONE
true owner`. A joint venture has **two or more principals behind one SPE**, each of whom we
prospect independently.

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **J1** | **Design the multi-owner ownership edge** — one SPE resolving to **N** true owners, each with a role (JV partner, GP/LP, DST beneficiary, sole owner) and ideally a share. Must not break the existing single-owner path, which is nearly all of the data. | 🟢 👤 | `entity_relationships` may already carry the shape; **check before adding a table** |
| **J2** | **`Boyd Watterson JV UBP` is the live worked example** — Scott approved it into the Boyd family, and it is a JV, so today it resolves to Boyd alone and the second partner (`UBP`?) is invisible. **Use it as the design's test case.** | 🟢 | V8 row 1 |
| **J3** | **Leasehold vs fee is a REAL ownership split, not a weaker claim.** Scott: a `Lessee` SPE holds the **leasehold** (improvements) under a ground lease while another party holds the **fee** (dirt); the leasehold SPE is the landlord counterparty to the tenant. `Cr Sunflower Lessee LLC` is the live example. **The model should distinguish fee / leasehold / both**, or a ground-leased asset silently reads as one owner when it has two. | 🟢 | V8 row 3 |
| **J4** | **Downstream consequence — prospecting.** If an asset has two JV principals, both belong in the pursuit surfaces, and neither should be suppressed because the other is recorded. Check what `v_lcc_owner_contact_worklist` and the Tier 0 lane do with a multi-owner asset **before** J1 ships. | 🔴 | own item |

## P10 — Known, sized, unfixed data defects (carried from `CLAUDE.md`)

These are *measured* problems with a *known* shape. They are not vague.

| # | Item | Size |
|---|---|---|
| K1 | **`lcc_reconcile_property_owner` scores an ownership CHAIN as competing claims.** Recency decay floored at 0.25 means a thrice-sold building yields three near-equal candidates under the 0.55 gate. **876 assets have evidence and still read "Unresolved."** A strict-latest-purchase supersession tier would resolve **465**, correctly abstaining on the 360 that tie on date. **Adding another feeder does not fix this class — don't reach for one first.** |
| K2 | **The SAM tier ladder vs the real quota.** Tiers 0 (131) and 1 (514) run before tier 2 — and **tier 2 holds 9,153 valued owners topping out at $62.3M**. Sized for 600/day; the key actually delivers ~10/day, so the $62.3M owner is ~2 years out. Either interleave value across tiers or raise the SAM account tier. **Scott's call.** |
| K3 | **`v_field_provenance_unranked` returns 35 rows** for tables other than `entities.email/phone` — pre-existing writer/ladder drift. Should be 0. |
| K4 | **`v_owner_contact_enrich_queue`'s exclusion never expires.** All 316 `owner_contact_manual` tasks are `queued` and none has ever changed status, so the owners are permanently removed from automated processing — while **115 of them ($102.4M) already carry a genuine named active contact.** *Ask what event sets this state false, and whether anything ever fires it.* |
| K5 | **`v_lcc_weak_reach_worklist`** — 93 owners ($153.8M of annual rent) gated out of "reachable" by P161 because their only route is a weak `works_at` edge. They are the contact-acquisition engine's queue, not a defect. |
| K6 | **`v_lcc_portfolio_ownership_conflict`** — 12 ghost-vs-survivor portfolio conflicts, never auto-resolved by design (deleting the ghost would resolve toward the stale side). |
| K7 | **gov firm-term tail** — 625 sold-cap chart rows still lack a term; ~178 are STATE/municipal (no federal register can cover them; the state-lease producer has been **silent since 2026-06-23**), and 58 properties are OCR-pending. Surfaced in `v_gov_firm_term_backfill_queue` / `_reextract_queue`. |
| K8 | **gov `listing_verification_history`** records `prior_asking_price` but leaves `asking_price_at_check`/`price_delta` NULL on 5,636/5,637 rows. The reprice triggers made the chart writer-agnostic, but fixing the lvh writer would enrich the ledger. |
| K9 | **gov FRPP↔property matching pass.** The CoStar VA/SSA comp tail was never linked to its FRPP row (address-match ceiling ~29 of 533 distinct properties). FRPP holds `lease_expiration_date` on 19,756 leased assets — a geo/address matching pass is the real-volume unlock. |
| K10 | **gov Phase A1b — per-county assessor fetchers.** Mass owner-mailing capture needs them; the AI-extraction path only yields "echoes" for counties whose assessor URL is a search portal. |
| K11 | **dia CMS `patient_month_backfill`** — must run on a CMS-reachable runner (the sandbox has no data.cms.gov egress). Loads whatever months the current survival window covers; never fabricates a period. |

### K13–K18 — the Capital-Markets chart defects (recovered 2026-08-27 from a root-level worklog)

⚠️ **These five were measured in detail on 2026-08-10/11 and never reached this backlog**, because
the P141 consolidation swept `docs/` and they lived in `CAPMARKETS_TAB_PACKET_WORKLOG.md` at the
repo **root**. Recovered while consolidating the root worklogs; source now archived at
`docs/history/worklogs/CAPMARKETS_TAB_PACKET_WORKLOG.md`. **Each carries its own live measurement —
re-measure before acting, but do not re-derive.**

| # | Item | Size |
|---|---|---|
| K13 | **`cm_gov_sold_cap_by_term_dot` uses the OLD term ladder, not `firm_term_years_at_sale`.** It prioritises `(gsa_leases.termination_date - sale_date)`, then `leases.firm_term_years - elapsed`, then the older `sales_transactions.firm_term_years` — the **opposite** of the dia R68/R66x pattern where the sale-frozen field is canonical. **1,368 cap-eligible sales bucket differently** under the two ladders; 2025–26 sales with 10+ years at sale plot as `<5` or `outside` because a stale GSA termination date wins first. Term data is NOT missing (3,211/3,211 populated). **Also the view's `cap_5to10` cohort is labelled `6-10`**, so 5–6yr sales sit in the middle bucket behind a wrong label. Fix: `COALESCE(firm_term_years_at_sale, lease fallback only when null)`, align buckets to 10+/6-10/<6/outside, keep the 2yr TTM + n>=5 gate, expose per-cohort n. **This is a stale view definition, not a data-ingestion failure.** |
| K14 | **`cm_gov_lease_termination_rate_m/_q` can select a CORRUPT partial snapshot as its active denominator.** `gsa_snapshots` 2019-02 holds **11 lease keys** against ~8,050 in adjacent months, so Feb-2019 plots `total_leases_active=11`. The view already builds a `clean_snap` set for departure endpoints but the active count still carries forward from raw `snap_agg`. Fix: carry forward the latest **clean** snapshot on/before the period using the same plausibility guard. |
| K15 | **Corrupt source snapshot months need re-ingest/repair** — `gsa_snapshots` 2019-02; `gsa_inventory_snapshot_lines` 2019-02, 2022-10, 2022-11. Separate from K14: fixing the view stops this chart hitting them, but every other consumer still can. |
| K16 | **`cm_gov_rent_price_psf_q` has no display policy and pre-1997 history is not reliable.** 1990–96 has `rent_psf` null throughout (`n_with_rent_ttm=0`) while `price_psf` rides 3–4 TTM observations (1994-12 ≈ $638/SF, 1995-12 ≈ $528/SF). First sustained period by a conservative gate (8 consecutive quarters, both series populated, both n>=4) is **1997-06-30**; density is materially better from 2003 and strong 2005+. No `rent_and_price_psf` row exists in the gov `cm_view_registry`, so nothing is cropped today. **Scott's call: 1997-06-30 for maximum history, or 2003-01-01 to prioritise sample density.** |
| K17 | **`cm_gov_market_turnover_m` — export-side crop applied in code, DB and export policy have not converged.** Pre-2012 rows are artifacts (2005-01 `active_count=1`, 2008-12 `=2`) against 79–180 TTM sales. A curated `resolveEffectiveDisplayFrom()` fallback pins gov market_turnover at 2012-01-01 in the exporter, **because the live gov `cm_view_registry` has no `display_from` column** (`42703`). Fix: migrate the registry columns or crop in the view, so source and export agree. |
| K19 | **gov seller-sentiment fix was never mirrored to dia.** `cm-excel-export.js` drops the `_8q` columns and relabels `10+ yr` → `6+ yr` for gov; the 2026-08-12 follow-up ("mirror the fix to dia + recent-tail coverage guard") is recorded as intent with no evidence it landed. Re-measure before acting. → `docs/history/worklogs/GOVERNMENT_SELLER_SENTIMENT_WORKLOG.md` |
| K20 | **dia 23654 Census-radius demographics write never completed.** Prompt 16's live-apply checklist items 1–2 were applied and re-verified; **item 3 (the Census-radius demographics write) is the remaining one.** `scripts/backfill-dia-location-trade-area-23654.mjs` passes syntax check and `.env.local` carries a `CENSUS_API_KEY`. Related to the un-re-measured "Census key" note carried in `CURRENT-STATE.md` §7. → `docs/history/worklogs/DOSSIER_LOCATION_TRADE_AREA_23654_WORKLOG.md` |
| K18 | **`cm_gov_core_cap_rate_dots` retains a lease-derived term fallback that nothing currently uses.** Audited 2026-08-11: all 1,143 plotted dots come from sale-frozen `firm_term_years_at_sale`; `lease_fallback_term_dots = 0`. **Acceptable for current report use** — but future unbackfilled sales could enter through the fallback. Fix if stricter provenance is wanted: expose a term-source flag, or require `firm_term_years_at_sale IS NOT NULL` in the core-dot wrapper. |

## P15 — dia ownership lane: coverage, buyer→company linkage, pattern precision (measured 2026-08-29)

Filed from `docs/audits/DIA_OWNERSHIP_LANE_COVERAGE_2026-08-29.md`, written after the
Deals ▸ Ownership statement-timeout fix made the lane render. **Everything below is measured,
nothing is built.** The lane shows 16 canonicals / 61 variants / 500 properties; the audit
explains what that is a fraction *of*.

⚠️ **The one thing to carry forward if you read nothing else:**
`v_recorded_owner_canonical_clusters` is a readout of the **38-row hand-curated
`owner_canonical_patterns` table**, not a detector. `dia_canonicalize_owner_name` returns the
name unchanged when no pattern matches, and there are **ZERO byte-identical duplicate
`recorded_owners.name` values** — so **72 of 7,255 owners (1.0%) can ever appear**, and 100%
of what shows is the patterns showing themselves. The panel copy has been corrected to say so.

| # | Item | State | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OWN1** | 👤 **Two `owner_canonical_patterns` regexes over-capture, and one wrong link is ALREADY WRITTEN.** `^healthcare\s+realty(\s+trust)?` has no end anchor, so **`HealthCare Realty Solutions`** canonicalizes to `Healthcare Realty Trust` — a different company, and tellingly the one *unlinked* member of that cluster. `^(sumitomo\s+bank\s+leasing\|smbc\s+leasing\|sumitomo\s+mitsui)` matches any `sumitomo mitsui …`, so **`Sumitomo Mitsui Trust Bank`** (Sumitomo Mitsui **Trust** Holdings — a different corporate group from SMBC/SMFG) is folded into SMBC **and already carries SMBC's `true_owner_id`**. | ✅ **J13a-guard** | **SHIPPED 2026-09-08 — the retired-host class is now a CI test, not a fourth sweep.** `test/retired-identifiers-guard.test.mjs` scans every tracked file (`git ls-files`, all types, case-insensitive, JS/TS comment-stripped) against `test/fixtures/retired-identifiers.json` (`life-command-center-nine.vercel.app`, `docs/os/architecture/`, `GOV_STATE_SIGNALS`), failing on any hit outside `docs/history/`/`docs/archive/`/`docs/audits/`/`docs/claude-code/`/a `STALE (DOCMAP…`/`RETIRED`-bannered doc/a named allowlist entry — positive-controlled both directions, seen RED (allowlist entry removed) and green. **`life-command-center-production.up.railway.app` was DROPPED from the fixture, not shipped** — it is referenced as a live fallback default in six `api/*.js` files with no measurement that it is actually dormant; asserting it retired without verifying would have been exactly the class of wrong dated-blocker claim this file warns against (backlog note, not filed as its own row — re-measure before adding it). Unit 2 moved the machine-read artifacts J13a found (3 root `flow-*.json`, the Copilot Studio `manifest.json`/`ai-plugin.json`/`LCC-Assistant.zip`, and the `lcc-agent/appPackage/{manifest.json,build/manifest.dev.json,build/appPackage.dev.zip}` Teams-Toolkit build outputs — none hand-edited) to `docs/archive/retired-vercel-artifacts/` with a README naming each live equivalent; `scripts/build_canonical_connector.py` reads only the sibling `openapi.json` files, which never carried the host and were not moved. Allowlisted (pre-existing, cannot fix in this change): `wave0-config-values.txt` (SEC2) and `test/sf-deal-promotion.test.mjs` (asserts `GOV_STATE_SIGNALS` no longer exists — naming it IS the regression check). Does NOT tear the Vercel deployment down (J13 👤) or repoint any live PA flow. ✅ **VERIFIED + TIGHTENED (Cowork, 2026-09-08 21:5x UTC).** Ran locally 6/6 → RED on a synthetic tracked offender in `api/` (`hits=1`, file named) → green. 🚨 **Then found a hole and closed it:** the banner exemption was `/STALE \(DOCMAP|RETIRED/i` over the first 40 lines of ANY file — 40+ tracked files rode the bare word "retired" (live `api/_shared/*.js`, `dc-lanes.js`, four `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `CURRENT-STATE.md`, `AGENTS.md`), and **a hardcoded retired host appended to `api/_shared/share-extractor.js` left the suite GREEN** — the P194 shape exactly. Now `.md`-only and blockquote-only (`> … STALE (DOCMAP` / `> … RETIRED`); two positive controls added (8 tests); the same probe now FAILS naming the file; three correctly-framed docs the word had been carrying are exempted BY PATH with reasons (`INTAKE_TODO_FLOW_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md` — a dated audit that belongs in `docs/audits/`, move candidate; the 2026-08-11 CODEX triage prompt; `DOCMAP1_CLASSIFICATION.md`). Final: `tracked=4486 scanned=4403 hits=0 exempt=86 allowlisted=2`. **Lesson: a word is not a banner — an exemption must be shaped like the artifact it excuses.** | ✅ | J13a · DOCMAP3 reconcile |

## P17 — 🏘️ PROPERTY DATA RECONCILIATION REVIEW (Scott, 2026-09-10) — DaVita Kidney Care, Donna TX walkthrough

> Scott walked one property (a clinic he personally sold in 2017-18, now re-listed) across every tab
> of the LCC app and attached 9 screenshots + narrative, asking two explicit questions: are there
> **multiple unmerged DaVita records for this one Donna, TX property**, or is the app **not fully
> connecting/ingesting data from sources it already has**? Investigated directly against live LCC Opps
> (`xengecqvemvfknjvbvrq`) before writing any row below — **both are true, and they are the same root
> cause.** Source notes: `docs/claude-code/responses/done/property-review-2026-09-10-davita-donna-tx.md`.

**The root cause (PDR1) explains most of the rest.** Five `entities` rows exist for this one physical
property/city, never merged: `c94991a3…` ("Donna, TX", domain `dia`, no address — a bare city
placeholder), `d90be440…` ("1006 I-2, Donna, TX 78537", domain `dia`, address populated, normalized),
`3c2dc7d3…` ("1006 E INTERSTATE 2", domain `dia`, address populated but **never normalized** —
`normalized_address` is NULL, city stored `DONNA` not `Donna`), `8d1fd46e…` ("DaVita - Donna, TX",
**domain NULL**, no address at all — this is the record the listing page actually opens), and
`9e6ce72a…` ("303 E Wacker Dr, Suite 1111, Chicago, IL 60601", city **incorrectly** set to `Donna,
TX` while the address is Northmarq's Chicago office — a distinct data-quality defect, not a duplicate).
The record the UI opens (`8d1fd46e…`) carries its own explanation in `metadata`: it was minted by the
**Salesforce opportunity-sync** ingestion (`sf_opp_id: 0068W00000jefuuQAA`), flagged
`orphan_flagged: true` / `ambiguous_resolution: [c94991a3…, d90be440…, 3c2dc7d3…]` at creation on
**2026-07-29** — the sync itself could not tell which of the three prior candidates was the real
property and correctly said so — **and nothing has resolved it in the 43 days since.** This is not a
missing subsystem: `mcp/entity-reconcile.js` → `GET/POST /api/pipeline/flagged-deals` +
`POST /api/pipeline/reconcile-entity` (`rpc/reconcile_entity`) is a real, live, mounted endpoint pair
built for exactly this queue (per `docs/architecture/entity-reconciliation-design.md`) — it has just
never been run against this deal, and **there is no recurring cron or Decision Center lane that drains
it automatically**; every prior clearance (238→197, "sweep v3") was a manual one-time script pass.
`entities.metadata.orphan_flagged` (a *different*, generic dead-weight-graph flag, refreshed daily by
`lcc_refresh_orphan_flags()` via `pg_cron`) is read only as a contact-picker exclusion filter in two
files — it has never fed a merge/resolution path either. **Every one of PDR2–PDR8 below traces back to
the app operating on the wrong (orphan) entity, which has zero ownership/lease/document/deal links
because it was never pointed at the canonical property.**

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PDR1** | ✅ **BUILT + LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-10 — planner + flag-gated auto-merge tick + Decision Center lane, per Scott's P13#1 decision ("auto-merge the clear cases, queue the rest").** `api/_shared/ambiguous-entity-merge-planner.js` (pure scoring, documented threshold: one candidate ≥100 pts — has an address — leading the runner-up by ≥50 pts) + `api/_handlers/ambiguous-entity-automerge-tick.js` (GET ungated dry run / POST gated `AMBIGUOUS_ENTITY_AUTOMERGE`, off by default) + the new `ambiguous_entity_resolution` Decision Center lane, both routing every write through the SAME `rpc/reconcile_entity` — no second merge writer. ✅ **The real split, run live against production 2026-09-10 (Cowork, re-implementing the planner's exact scoring in SQL): of 189 entities, 22 auto-mergeable / 167 needs_human (`margin_too_close`) / 0 with no candidate clearing the minimum score.** DaVita/Donna-TX itself resolves correctly and is one of the 22 — winner `d90be440…` ("1006 I-2, Donna, TX 78537", score 172: addressed + normalized + 11 real signal rows) over runner-up `3c2dc7d3…` (score 104: addressed but not normalized) and the bare-city placeholder `c94991a3…` (score 40, no address at all) — exactly the ranking PDR1's own worked example called for. Migration `20260910120000` (run-log table + flag seed) **applied live** (Cowork); `AMBIGUOUS_ENTITY_AUTOMERGE` registered, state `off`, notes carry the real split. No recurring cron (population is closed, nothing minted since 2026-08-04). ✅ **EXECUTED 2026-09-10 (Scott: "run all 22 now") — 19 of 22 merged live via direct `reconcile_entity` calls (run log id 1, `lcc_ambiguous_entity_automerge_run_log`), all 19 `ok:true`.** ⚠️ **3 held back and routed to the review queue instead: the Rock Hill, SC cluster (Kohl's / Hobby Lobby and Shops / 1522-1526 Meeting Blvd) — their `ambiguous_resolution` candidate lists reference EACH OTHER as candidates, a case the scoring planner's address/signal check doesn't detect** (a candidate that is itself another unresolved placeholder can still score highest on address quality). Auto-merging these 3 could conflate up to three separate deals into one asset — filed as **PDR12** below, a real gap in the planner found during execution, not assumed safe. See STATUS.md 2026-09-10 for the full execution record, including DaVita/Donna-TX's confirmed downstream effect (PDR2/PDR4/PDR6/PDR7). | ✅ | shipped + live-verified + executed |
| **PDR2** | ❌ **Root cause found live 2026-09-11 (read-only investigation, no writes) — and it is systemic, not one-off.** `properties.property_id=37722.true_owner_id` already points at a `true_owners` row for "DaVita Kidney Care" that IS correctly flagged (`is_operator_not_owner=true`, `owner_role='operator'`) — the flag is right. The bug is a READ-path gap: `api/operations.js`'s `assemblePropertyPacket()` (~lines 8811–8829, the ownership block behind `get_property_context`) joins `true_owner_id → true_owners.name` **unconditionally**, ignoring the flag — unlike two other code paths in this same repo that already guard it correctly: `api/_handlers/entities-handler.js`'s `assemblePropertyDossier` §1.6 (~lines 742–751, nulls an operator-flagged owner and falls back to `recorded_owner_name`) and `api/_handlers/sf-link-reconcile.js`'s `isOperator()` (~line 270). **Blast radius measured: 1,182 properties nationwide point at this exact DaVita placeholder row; 4,026 properties fleet-wide have `recorded_owner_id IS NULL` and `true_owner_id` pointing at ANY `is_operator_not_owner=true` row** — this is a guard-gap bug affecting thousands of properties, not a DaVita-specific defect. Also layered under a deeper shared cause — see **PDR13**. Fix: close the guard gap in `assemblePropertyPacket` (and any other unguarded true-owner reader found while fixing it), sized against the real 4,026-property blast radius, not just this one property. | 🔴 | root cause found 2026-09-11, needs own prompt (larger scope than DaVita alone) |
| **PDR3** | ✅ **CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-09-11 — PDR14b closed the LCC-side gap.** The Feb-2019 sale (`sale_id 311`, $3,639,317, buyer Phil Decarion — **not "2017-18" as recalled**, no trace of an earlier sale anywhere) is on the merged dia property (`39874`); `bd_opportunities` already linked the deal to the LCC entity correctly all along. PDR14b repointed entity `d90be440…`'s `metadata.domain_property_id` `37722 → 39874` (via PDR14a's `dia_resolve_property_id` redirect), and `get_property_context` now returns the transaction live — confirmed by direct query, not inferred: `sales_transactions where property_id=39874` reads 1 row. | ✅ | confirmed live 2026-09-11 |

PDR14b repointed entity `d90be440…` to `39874` (same fix as PDR3), so `get_property_context` now resolves the CMS link — confirmed live: `properties.recorded_owner_id`/`true_owner_id` for `39874` both non-null, and the entity's `domain_property_id` reads `39874`. | ✅ | confirmed live 2026-09-11 |

Still missing the 2017-18 sale history and any pre-2026 Team Briggs touches — bounded by PDR3's ingestion gap, not a further entity-identity issue. Catalogued per Scott's "catalog all errors found," but this one is already honestly labeled in the UI and does not need a new fix — ⚠️ **PDR1's code is built but NOT yet run live** — if it later produces a canonical entity with a clean address, geocoding may simply succeed on the next pass. Re-check after an actual live merge, no dedicated build unless it's still ungeocoded with a good address in hand. | 🟢 | depends on PDR1 — awaiting live run |

Likely a broker/listing-agent address field bleeding into the asset record's city column during some ingestion path (CoStar sidebar capture or SF sync are the two candidates given this session's other findings — not yet isolated). Worth a short, separate investigation once PDR1 is resolved, since it may point at a real field-mapping bug in whichever writer produced it. | 🔴 | found during PDR1 investigation |
| **PDR11** | **The systemic question underneath all of the above, now answered with live data, not assumed:** duplicate/unmerged records AND incomplete ingestion are BOTH real, and they are downstream of the SAME mechanism gap — Salesforce opportunity-sync mints an honest placeholder when it can't disambiguate an address (`ambiguous_resolution`), a real reconciliation endpoint exists to resolve it, and nothing runs that endpoint on a schedule. **The fleet-wide fix that prevents the next DaVita-Donna from happening is PDR1(b), not a per-property patch** — measure the population of open `ambiguous_resolution` placeholders fleet-wide next, before building the sweep. | 🔴 | PDR1 |
| **PDR12** | **Found during PDR1 execution 2026-09-10 — a real gap in the auto-merge scoring planner.** 3 of the 189 ambiguous entities (Kohl's / Hobby Lobby and Shops / 1522-1526 Meeting Blvd — all Rock Hill, SC) have `ambiguous_resolution` candidate lists that reference EACH OTHER, not just real canonical property candidates. The planner's address/normalization/signal scoring still picks a winner (the one real addressed candidate, "2670 Mills Park Dr"), but merging three differently-named deals into one asset risks conflating genuinely separate businesses/deals unless they really do share one physical address (plausible — a shopping-center anchor + shops — but not verified). Held back from the 2026-09-10 execution and routed to the `ambiguous_entity_resolution` Decision Center lane for a human look. **Fix for the planner: detect when a candidate id is itself another `ambiguous_resolution`-flagged entity and force `needs_human` regardless of score** — a small, targeted addition, not a rebuild. | 🔴 | found live 2026-09-10 |
| **PDR13** | ✅ **SHIPPED + LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-11 (Cowork independently re-queried every claim against live Dialysis_DB before trusting the shipped commit message).** Dialysis-repo session shipped a second, address-independent twin detector (`dia_find_property_twins_strong_id` / `dia_merge_strong_id_twins`, migration `20260911123900_dia_pdr13_property_twins_strong_id.sql`) keyed on `parcel_number` (len ≥6) UNION effective `medicare_id` (CCN len=6, `COALESCE(property_cms_link.medicare_id, properties.medicare_id)`), reusing the SAME `dia_property_twin_review` lane + `dia_merge_property_reversible` round trip the existing geospatial detector uses — no second merge mechanism. **Real fleet-wide population, confirmed live: 48 pairs (32 `review_name` / 16 `review_conflict` / 0 `auto_blank` / 0 `review_ambiguous`).** **The Donna, TX group specifically confirmed merged**: `properties` now holds exactly 2 rows (`39874` canonical, `45543` — a genuine address data-entry error, correctly left alone), `sales_transactions` for the group is exactly 1 row (`sale_id 311`, $3,639,317, 2019-02-01, now on `39874`), `property_cms_link` still correctly on `39874`. `dia_property_merge_backup` rows `587/588/589` confirmed live with the exact nested `rewired` payload the response claimed — checked at the JSON-payload level, not assumed from the response's prose. **⚠️ New gap found during this verification, out of PDR13's own scope (an LCC-side gap, not dia-side): see PDR14.** | ✅ | shipped + live-verified 2026-09-11 |
| **PDR14** | ✅ **SHIPPED (dia side, PDR14a) + LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-11 — independently re-checked every claim against live Dialysis_DB before trusting the shipped response.** Dialysis-repo session shipped `dia_property_redirects` (`dropped_property_id, kept_property_id, merged_at, source, batch_tag, note, reversed_at`) + `dia_resolve_property_id(bigint) returns bigint` (SECURITY DEFINER, `service_role`-only — confirmed via `has_function_privilege`) + `v_dia_property_redirect_resolved`, migration `20260911120000_dia_pdr14a_property_redirects.sql`, applied live. **Full census of dia's merge ledgers** (measured, spot-checked): `dia_property_merge_backup` 588 rows, `property_merge_log` 656 rows, `p31_property_consolidation_log` 12 rows, `dia_property_consolidation_log` 6 rows, `dq7_property_merge_map` 19 rows — backfilled into `dia_property_redirects` as 1,267 deduplicated active redirects (confirmed live: `count(*)=1267, active=1267`). **Corrected orphan split (re-derived independently against my own 89-item orphan list, not the earlier 28/61 estimate): 31 of 89 resolve via `dia_resolve_property_id`, 58 do not** — the 3-item difference from my own 28 explained the earlier ledger-tally missed chained resolution and a soft-merge column (`properties.merged_into_property_id`) no single ledger could see. DaVita/Donna-TX positive control confirmed live: `dia_resolve_property_id(37722)=39874, (23545)=39874, (37710)=39874`. **Design correction from my own PDR14a prompt's assumption:** the redirect-write hook lives in the shared primitive `dia_merge_property` (not `dia_merge_property_reversible`) because the geospatial cron calls the primitive directly, bypassing the reversible wrapper — six merge paths confirmed hooked in total. Guard: 30 passed/2 skipped, 18/18 mutations RED. No PR opened; committed direct to a feature branch, applied live. **PDR14b (life-command-center side) is now unblocked** — sweep + fallback re-resolution + ongoing monitoring, calling `dia_resolve_property_id` via `domainQuery('dialysis', …)` (service-key path; NOT the anon-keyed `data-query` edge function). ⚠️ **58 of 89 still have no redirect trace and remain unexplained — PDR14a's own scope deliberately did not chase them further; PDR14b's fallback step (confident address/parcel/CCN re-resolution, mirroring PDR13) is the intended path for those, not more archaeology.** See **PDR14-GOV** below for the parallel investigation on the government side. ✅ **PDR14b SHIPPED + APPLIED LIVE 2026-09-11 (life-command-center repo).** Real split, re-derived independently against `dia_resolve_property_id` (89 distinct dead pids / **90 dangling entities** — one dead pid, `29100`, shared by two entities, invisible to a pid-level count): **31 via redirect** (matches PDR14a's own number exactly) / **13 via an unambiguous PDR13-style parcel_number match** (exact match, single candidate, `min length 6` — the same default as `dia_find_property_twins_strong_id`; one `n_match=3` candidate at `parcel='14'` was correctly refused by that length floor, not resolved) / **46 flagged** into the new `lcc_dia_property_link_review` table, never guessed (no medicare_id/CCN fallback was possible for the residue — none carry one, a real ceiling). Applied via a single merge-owner RPC (`lcc_pdr14b_apply_dia_redirect`, SECURITY DEFINER + revoke/assert stanza) rather than a PostgREST PATCH, because `entities.metadata` is a shared jsonb column (the OCR2 footgun). Migrations `20260911190000_lcc_pdr14b_dia_property_link_review.sql` + `…190100_…one_time_sweep_corrections.sql`. Ongoing monitoring: `GET/POST /api/dia-property-link-tick` (flag `PDR14B_DIA_REDIRECT_SWEEP`, **on**), a recurring sweep chosen over inline self-heal in `get_property_context`'s hot read path — reasoning + tests (`test/pdr14b-dia-property-redirect.test.mjs`, 15 tests) in `docs/claude-code/STATUS.md` 2026-09-11. **Acceptance test confirmed live**: entity `d90be440…` now reads `domain_property_id=39874`; property `39874` carries 7 documents / 1 transaction / both owner ids populated. See PDR3/PDR6 above, now both ✅. | 🟢 | PDR14a + PDR14b both shipped + verified live 2026-09-11 |

> on-box Ollama Analyst's Take) — no second email engine. Private corpora never go to a cloud model.

## P10a — Cross-lane property identity and address resolution (designed, not built)

The restricted ASC sample exposed a platform class: property identity cannot be maintained as an expanding
set of lane-local string replacements. Canonical contract:
[`property-identity-and-address-resolution.md`](../architecture/property-identity-and-address-resolution.md).
All rows are aggregate-only and preserve the current fail-closed research boundary.

| # | Item | State | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| PI1 | **Inventory and version the existing ASC identity rules.** Name each transformation, evidence burden, reason code, collision guard, second-review requirement, and owner. Preserve historical frozen-token compatibility as an explicit rule, not an implicit re-normalization. | 🟢 | property identity contract §5–7; restricted ASC observations 2026-08-28 |
| PI2 | **Build a de-identified golden mismatch corpus with adversarial negative controls.** Cover suites/floors, parent buildings, same-address campuses, ranges, directionals, suffixes, municipality aliases, compound streets, postal variants, historical tokens, and confusable facility names. No private rows or licensed payloads in Git. | 🟢 | property identity contract §7 |
| PI3 | **Extract a lane-neutral pure matcher and structured decision object.** The component must be deterministic, versioned, write-free, and fail closed; existing ASC outcomes replay unchanged except separately approved corrections. | 🟢 | property identity contract §4–6 |
| PI4 | **Add sidebar identity diagnostics and explicit candidate refresh/advance state.** Show the active frozen candidate, component verdicts, corroboration, rule version, second-review flag, failure class, and one safe next action. Eliminate stale-candidate ambiguity without retargeting on page navigation. | 🟢 | property identity contract §8 |
| PI5 | **Run read-only shadow evaluation across approved current and future lanes.** Report changed decisions, collisions, and review load before any lane adopts a shared rule. No canonical writes or scope expansion. | 🟢 | property identity contract §7, §11 |
| PI6 | **Design a governed property-alias ledger.** Every alias needs citations, scope, authorization, timestamps, reason, review/expiry behavior, replay, and rollback. This requires a separate schema/security decision before migration or use. | 🔴 👤 | property identity contract §5, §11 |
| PI7 | **Emit aggregate identity-quality metrics.** Measure match modes, missingness, disagreements, collisions, second-review agreement, stale-state errors, technical failures, and research time without exposing row-level or licensed data. | 🟢 | property identity contract §10 |
| PI8 | **Optional on-box discrepancy-note assistant.** After PI3 emits structured decisions, an on-box model may suggest reason codes or draft reviewer notes. It is advisory only and may never match, alias, approve, or write. | ⚪ | property identity contract §9 |

**Gate:** complete and aggregate-review the frozen 50-property ASC sample before Phase B/PI2–PI3 begins.
Activation in any lane remains a separate decision.

## P12 — Explicitly excluded (kept so they are not re-proposed)

| Item | Why |
|---|---|
| **Work IQ Mail / Teams connectors** | Excluded by decision — email and comms stay on the LCC path. |
| **A PATCH projector for the 2,809 Outlook contacts** | Measured: ~144 field-values, most of which came *from* Outlook. Would run green and move nothing. |
| **Any CAPTCHA-solving service for SOS egress** | Standing prohibition. The unlock is client fidelity (cookie jar + browser-grade TLS), not defeating a challenge. |
| **Scraping LinkedIn** | ToS. Use the user's own connections export, company team pages, SEC filings, or a licensed API. |
| **Name-keyed web/LinkedIn enrichment** | Confidently moves people to the wrong firm (a 2026-08-26 search returned a *different* Andrew Pulliam). Key on email domain + employer corroboration, never a name match. |
| **`Deal_Participants__c` / SF OpportunityContactRole as the deal-party roster** | Verified dead end for Team Briggs — OCR is empty on all 592 backbone deals; `Deal_Participants__c.Deal` is `Fannie_Mae_Deal__c`. The correspondence-driven matcher is the roster. |

## P13 — Decision forks (Scott's call; do not build past them)

1. **Entity reconciliation** — 232 deals in multi-asset cities are parked as flagged entities. Auto-merge
   on best-match with a review queue, require manual confirmation for all 232, or treat the flagged
   entity as canonical and merge lazily as signal arrives? ⚠️ **Re-measured 2026-09-10 — this is P17/PDR1's root cause, found from the opposite direction (a single property Scott flagged by hand).** Population today: **189** entities carry `ambiguous_resolution` (down from 232 — 43 resolved by ad hoc sweeps since), **116 also `orphan_flagged`**, all minted in one **2026-07-28 to 2026-08-04** Salesforce-sync burst (nothing since). Candidate-list size per entity ranges 2 to 55 (median in the single digits) — some are trivially resolvable (a 2-candidate ambiguity where one candidate is a bare city placeholder, as DaVita/Donna-TX turned out to be), others genuinely need a human look. The live `flagged-deals`/`reconcile-entity` endpoint pair (`entity-reconcile.js`) can action any of the three strategies below — it is a question of WHICH strategy and how much to automate, not whether the tooling exists. See `PLANNED-BACKLOG.md` §P17 for the single-property case that surfaced this.
1. ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-10, PENDING LIVE VERIFICATION — Entity reconciliation.** Scott decided: **auto-merge
   the clear cases, queue the rest** (not require manual confirmation for all 189, not treat the flagged
   entity as canonical-and-lazy). Re-measured 2026-09-10 — this is P17/PDR1's root cause, found from the
   opposite direction (a single property Scott flagged by hand). Population at decision time: **189**
   entities carry `ambiguous_resolution` (down from 232 — 43 resolved by ad hoc sweeps since), **116 also
   `orphan_flagged`**, all minted in one **2026-07-28 to 2026-08-04** Salesforce-sync burst (nothing
   since). Candidate-list size per entity ranges 2 to 55 (median in the single digits). Built: a pure
   scoring planner (`api/_shared/ambiguous-entity-merge-planner.js`, documented threshold — one candidate
   with an address leading the runner-up by ≥50 pts), a flag-gated auto-merge tick
   (`api/_handlers/ambiguous-entity-automerge-tick.js`, `AMBIGUOUS_ENTITY_AUTOMERGE`, off), and the new
   `ambiguous_entity_resolution` Decision Center lane for the rest — both write paths reuse the SAME
   `rpc/reconcile_entity`, no second merge writer. ✅ **Real split measured live 2026-09-10 (Cowork): 22
   auto-mergeable / 167 needs_human / 0 uncleared, out of 189** — the flag (`AMBIGUOUS_ENTITY_AUTOMERGE`)
   stays OFF pending Scott's go-ahead to run the write path; the needs_human 167 route to the new
   Decision Center lane regardless. No recurring cron (closed population, per Scott's own scoping).
   **Decided AND built AND verified — this fork is closed pending only the flip decision below.** See
   STATUS.md 2026-09-10 and `PLANNED-BACKLOG.md` §P17 (PDR1) for the full record.
2. **Team mailbox intake (Kelly / Sarah / Nate)** — the single biggest cadence-accuracy lever (LCC sees
   only Scott's mailbox; every outlook row is `SYSTEM_ACTOR`). Which mailboxes, what auth model, how to
   attribute per broker. **Deferred by Scott.** Phase 1 (attribution) is buildable independently.
3. **Per-broker delivery (owner-scoped digests)** — live in the engine, specced as a PA flow,
   **PARKED by Scott** until build-out is done and errors are triaged.
4. **Cadence tiering: computed vs manual** (the D12 red line).
5. **The clean-assist rank scales** (N1) — re-ranking `dia_xref` also re-orders the human-facing
   Decision Center lane.
6. **SAM account tier vs interleaving value across tiers** (K2).

---

### How to keep this file honest

- When a row ships, **move it to `CURRENT-STATE.md` §2 and delete it here** — do not leave a ✅ row,
  that is how a backlog rots into a changelog.
- When a row is genuinely retired, **move it to P12 with the reason**, never delete it.
- When measurement refutes a row's premise (it happens often here), **rewrite the row with the
  measurement** rather than silently dropping it — the correction is usually the more valuable artifact.
